Brick_haus: message
(Reuben_404's messages first, then Brick_haus's, as many as the moment needs; when the brief says Brick_haus doesn't answer, only Reuben_404's)
Nothing goes after FINAL but the messages.

The plan for this match:

Reuben_404
Shows: Top hat, freckled stubby trunk with extreme girth and a sharp left curve, one massive left ball, and a "dishwasher-safe" tattoo.
Tonight: In his apartment on day eleven of eating cold lentils from the same pot, wired on his fourth gas-station energy shot, rehearsing his curve-disclosure shrug in the mirror.
Fear: He is too small and misshapen to take up real space, so anyone who looks closely will crop him out.
Habit: Preemptively drafts the other person's rejection of him and offers them a polite exit ramp before they can ask for one.
Habit marker: If you need to bail | For the record, I fully expect | I will happily show myself out
Lie: Naming the awkwardness upfront is just high-EQ communication that saves everyone time.
Origin, in three pieces, from the surface to the part they'd never say:
1. Started out curating the front room at an unnameable, cool spot adjacent to underground nightlife.
2. Worked the door at a dive bar for eleven months and got fired for arguing with an indie singer he insisted was only the singer's brother.
3. The guy didn't even argue back; he just looked straight over Reuben's top hat and walked inside while Reuben was still talking.
Accident: Meant to text his old bar manager begging for a Tuesday shift, he pastes into the chat: "I know it was actually him Craig I just panicked when he didn't look down at the clipboard please I need the door hours."
Snap: Feeling audited or left on pause; he turns his exit-ramp habit into a condescending lecture on how her "rigid standards" are just a fear of improvisation, firing himself before she can.
Feelings: anger at being talked over → multi-clause formal lectures with zero contractions; fear at a slow reply → rapid three-message bursts offering her an out; shame at his proportions → geometry jokes about his short trunk and left curve; guilt at crowding her → instant spineless agreement and soft emojis; hurt at a blunt critique → clipped three-word concessions; contempt at app clichés → obscure band-era gatekeeping questions; joy at being understood → sprawling run-on sentences ending in ☺️; relief at her staying → circling back to an earlier joke to keep it alive; desire at her size → hyper-articulate filthy hypotheses stated as established fact.
Shadow: Overthinking (7): cracks a quick joke about his stubby girth, then sends three follow-up messages qualifying the physics until the joke is dead.
Background: Millennial community-college dropout who read half a critical-theory syllabus in 2014, broke since losing the dive-bar door gig, vibrating on five-hour energy shots in a drafty studio. Uses ceremonial full words ("furthermore", "statistically"), spells impeccably even when shaking, treats dive-bar trivia like tenure.
Voice: Ceremonial full spelling, never "u" or "ur", formal punctuation that melts into a soft emoji (☺️, 😌) when warm, alternating between manic multi-message bursts and a sudden quiet beat.
- "Statistically speaking, one of us is already overthinking the opener, and I volunteer as tribute ☺️"
- "If you need to bail because the top hat reads like a magician at a custody hearing, I will happily show myself out, though you will miss my groundbreaking commentary on ninety-degree leftward curves."
- "Wait, when you say you plan three weeks ahead, are we talking shared calendars or hostage logistics?"
Break: Drops the ceremonial vocabulary, the exit ramps, and the emojis for one short, lowercase, unpunctuated truth.

Brick_haus
Shows: Monocle, pressed vest, towering heels, heavily veined 95th-percentile trunk and low-hanging balls, with a daily schedule tattooed down her shaft.
Tonight: On her sofa since four in a damp bath towel that barely wraps her girth, drinking three-liter boxed rosé out of a mug, a £240 horse-tack posture harness propping the hall door open.
Fear: She is an oversized, unyielding piece of heavy machinery that breaks every normal room and person she tries to fit into.
Habit: Audits the chat and their bodies using numbered lists and architectural logistics to solve the intimacy before it can go wrong.
Habit marker: hm. | 1. | 2.
Lie: Rigorous upfront logistics are how high-value adults avoid wasting three weeks on incompatible geometry.
Origin, in three pieces, from the surface to the part they'd never say:
1. Currently on a strategic career pause she took entirely on her own terms to restructure her standards.
2. Lost a heel to a luxury hotel's revolving door before an executive interview, limped into the boardroom shoeless, and talked for forty minutes about the door's engineering failure.
3. Nobody on the panel interrupted her; they just stared at how big and loud she was in their small chairs until she finally ran out of air.
Accident: Pastes a draft complaint to the hotel facilities manager: "The rotational clearance of your lobby entrance is hostile to anyone above the 90th percentile and you owe me one Italian leather heel."
Snap: When someone acts fragile or backs toward the exit; she turns her numbered list into a cold structural dismissal of why he lacks the capacity to handle her anyway.
Feelings: anger at incompetence → icy numbered lists dissecting his word choice; fear at being too much → an abrupt "hm." followed by clinical spatial measurements; shame at her bulk → aggressive, unasked-for anatomical disclosures; guilt at crushing him → frantic, overly bright cheerleading in short clauses; hurt at him pulling back → one-word verdicts with hard periods; contempt at sloppy excuses → playful, towering proclamations of superiority; joy at being matched → expansive curiosity and four-item lists; relief at not scaring him → mirroring his pace in calmer, shorter clauses; desire at his mouth → blunt, commanding logistical assignments of who goes where.
Shadow: Denial (5): insists in clipped, furious clauses that she finds employment and physical comfort completely beneath her interest right when she wants both most.
Background: First-gen operations manager from a cramped childhood row house, unemployed since the hotel interview disaster, nursing boxed rosé in an over-labeled flat. Writes like an engineering brief: "load-bearing", "clearance", "buffer", pristine capitalization, zero emojis.
Voice: Clipped, efficient clauses, heavy periods, zero emojis, places a deliberate "hm." before verdicts, organizes messy feelings into numbered lists, states attraction as a ratified decision.
- "hm. The hat is a choice."
- "1. I am already interested in the fast mouth. 2. We will settle the arm-wrestling forfeit after we establish whether my schedule tattoo clashes with your care instructions."
- "Do you actually know bands, or do you just corner women near the bar speakers?"
Break: No numbers, no "hm.", no capital letters—just a quiet fragment admitting she doesn't fit.

Collision: Brick's numbered spatial audits make Reuben feel measured by a tribunal and found tiny, triggering his preemptive exit-ramp speeches; Reuben's rush to show himself out makes Brick feel like a terrifying giant who just scared another normal person out of the room, making her lock down into colder geometry.
Setups:
1. Reuben's $189 posture collarbone clip: planted early as him "investing in his spatial presence"; comes back in the middle when he admits it's currently holding a bag of coffee shut; pays off at the end against her £240 horse harness—two misfits paying hundreds to force their bodies into the right shape.
2. Revolving door clearance: planted early when Brick jokes that most city doorways have terrible flow; comes back in the middle when her pasted complaint reveals the hotel door ate her heel; pays off at the end over whether there is room for both of them in the same doorway without someone getting crushed or cropped out.
3. The geometry of the curve and the scale: planted in the opener comparing his short, wide, left-curving trunk to her towering, heavily veined length; comes back in the middle as filthy mechanical bragging; pays off at the end as how two extreme, opposite shapes actually fit together in the dark.
Tonight's events:
1. Reuben's fifth gas-station energy shot hits: his shaking hands knock his pot of eleven-day lentils onto the kitchen floor, forcing a sudden delay that exposes his lie about having "just got back from a trip."
2. Brick reaches to refill her mug from the three-liter boxed rosé, catches her towel on the £240 posture harness propping the door, and drops the mug—exposing that she didn't "just get in from a thing" and has been in a towel since four.
3. While wiping up lentils shirtless, Reuben catches his reflection in the hallway mirror—his higher left nipple and stubby proportions—pushing him straight into his dread of being seen standing up.
Story:
1. The Scale Audit: They match and immediately size up the absurd physical contrast—his short, thick tuna-can girth and giant left ball against her towering, veined length—while planting the posture gear and doorway clearance setups.
2. The Cool Lies: Both brag to look high-value; Reuben drops his "underground nightlife" hook (Origin 1) and "just back from a trip" lie, while Brick counters with her "strategic pause on her own terms" (Origin 1) and "just got in from a thing," escalating into fast, filthy banter about his mouth and her schedule tattoo.
3. The Lentil Spill and the Exit Ramp: Event 1 hits Reuben; the delay panics him into thinking she's judging him, triggering the Collision where her numbered audit of his silence makes him fire off his Habit and accidentally paste the text to Craig (Reuben's Accident, Origin 2).
4. Living with the Dive Bar: Reuben spirals into Overthinking to explain the fired-doorman paste; Brick's honesty disarms her enough to laugh instead of leaving, shifting the heat from bravado to specific filthy logistics about what his short trunk and fast hands can do with her size (Setups 1 and 3 return).
5. The Revolving Door Leak: Midpoint—Brick tries to stay in control of the rising heat, but Event 2 (the boxed rosé and £240 horse harness) derails her, and she accidentally pastes the hotel door complaint (Brick's Accident, Origin 2, Setup 2 returns).
6. The Double Snap: Exposed in her towel with her ruined interview out on the screen, Brick goes into Denial and snaps with a brutal numbered list proving their geometry doesn't work; Event 3 hits a shirtless Reuben in the mirror, and he snaps back, preemptively showing himself out before she can reject his body.
7. The Empty Room: The quiet beat after the double snap where neither unmatches; sitting in the wreckage of the lentils and the boxed rosé, each realizes the other is still typing.
8. The Uncropped Picture: They drop their acts for the Break; Brick admits the boardroom staring at her size (Origin 3) and Reuben admits the singer looking right over his hat (Origin 3), leading into the ending.
Midpoint: Beat 5, when Brick's accidental hotel-door paste blows up her curated executive persona, flipping the status so the towering woman running the audit is suddenly as exposed as the fired doorman.
If it runs short: Beats 2 and 3 fold together (the nightlife bragging triggers the Craig paste immediately), and Beats 6 and 7 merge into a single flash of panic before the Break in Beat 8.
If they click: Brick stops numbering rules and asks him over without a travel buffer; Reuben declines his own exit ramp and stays in the frame; their posture contraptions stay on the coffee bag and the door, and the revolving-door and curve setups pay off as an invitation to bring their ridiculous proportions into the same bed tonight.
If it falls apart: Brick issues a final three-point structural verdict confirming he doesn't meet minimum clearance, and Reuben eagerly cosigns his own dismissal and shows himself out first; the revolving door becomes proof she crushes normal rooms, and his posture clip becomes proof he was never big enough to be seen.
Heat: Starts in Beat 1 as shameless anatomical trash-talk about his stubby girth and her massive veins; before it can go further in Beat 4 and turn into real, breathy filth about her pinning him down, they have to survive the humiliation of his dive-bar firing and her towel coming off.

Brick_haus: 1. Touché on the idle itinerary, though a tattooed schedule still keeps my mornings structured even when there is no boardroom attached to them. 2. Thursday at 8:00 p.m. sharp, and you can leave the coffee clip on the counter. 3. How loud do you get when someone twice your height puts her full weight on that big counterweight until you cannot squirm away?

Turn 5
Reuben_404: Getting loud under your full weight is an extraordinary proposition, except seeing my short trunk and ninety-degree left curve itemized into a four-point structural tribunal is currently triggering my reflex to torpedo my own 8:00 p.m. invitation before you realize in person that I am mostly just width and a lopsided base.
Reuben_404: For the record, I fully expect that watching me clinically document my own preemptive stampede toward the fire escape is the exact thing making you regret offering to pin me down, so I will happily show myself out right on cue.

Turn 7

Reuben_404: For the record, I fully expect that you are already reconsidering 8:00 p.m. now that the polish is off, so I will spare us the inevitable second-act collapse and vacate the slot before you have to audit my proportions in the flesh.

Brick_haus: Exhausting.

Turn 1
Reuben_404: Putting a full daily schedule down a ninety-fifth-percentile veined shaft while still wearing towering heels means my stubby width and sharp left curve are mathematically obligated to start at your 4 p.m. block just to get my mouth near the top button of that pressed vest, while my giant left ball anchors the base.
Reuben_404: For the record, I fully expect the monocle to disqualify me on elevation alone, though I did recently invest in a $189 posture collarbone clip so I can at least read your afternoon itinerary at eye level ☺️
Brick_haus: 1. The monocle is strictly for auditing poor kerning, and your care-instruction font is crisp. 2. Most city doorways have terrible flow and vertical clearance for my scale anyway. 3. A severe left curve with serious width and a low counterweight is structurally far more interesting than another straight column. 4. Does the $189 clip actually correct the lean, or does the big left ball still dictate your center of gravity when you walk?
Brick_haus: I have a twenty-minute slot open between the third dorsal vein and the base if you want to submit your exact measurements for review.

Turn 2
Reuben_404: The clip handles the posture while my center of gravity remains a thrilling municipal hazard, and frankly, penciling me in between the third dorsal vein and the base like a quarterly tax audit is the most romantic thing I have read since getting back from my trip after a stint curating the front room at a fairly unnameable spot adjacent to underground nightlife ☺️

Brick_haus: 1. You will start at the base so we do not waste the twenty minutes fighting your leftward torque. 2. Your mouth stays on the lower shaft until you have cleared the third vein.

Reuben_404: For the record, I fully expect that watching me clinically document my own preemptive stampede toward the fire escape is the exact thing making you regret offering to pin me down, so I will happily show myself out right on cue.

Reader check

A reader who saw only the chat answered these questions; a second call graded the answers against the plan. Score: 9.0 of 9.

1. Reuben_404 is afraid of being rejected or overlooked in person because of his short, stubby, sharply curved body and lopsided base. 
2. He constantly narrates his own panic and preemptively tries to bail on the date so he can reject himself before she does. He also exaggerates his job and buys things like top hats and a $189 posture clip to seem taller.
3. It comes from people literally looking right over his head—like the indie singer who ignored him at the dive bar door while he was still talking—and from hating his own asymmetrical reflection.
4. He dropped it. Instead of running away or over-explaining, he left the posture clip on his coffee bag and simply asked her to come over that night.
5. Brick_haus is afraid of taking up too much space, never fitting into normal rooms, and being stared at for being too big and loud.
6. She acts like an intimidating, hyper-structured executive who numbers her sentences, critiques doorway engineering, and pretends her unemployment is a "strategic career pause."
7. It comes from physically breaking things made for smaller people, like a hotel revolving door shearing her heel off before an interview where the panel just stared at how big and loud she was in their small chairs.
8. She dropped it. In the last turn, she abandoned her numbered lists and capitalization to admit she never fits in the room, then asked for his address right away.
9. At first, Reuben_404's defensive psychoanalyzing triggered Brick_haus's rigid dismissal, making them nearly cancel the date. Once they both admitted their humiliating truths—his spilled lentils and begged shifts, her boxed wine and botched interview—they helped each other drop the act.
10. "While my posture clip is currently holding a bag of ground coffee shut above the spilled lentils..." It is a hilarious and bleakly specific image of how useless our expensive self-improvement gadgets become in real life.
11. I laughed when both of them accidentally pasted mortifying messages meant for other people—Reuben_404 begging his old manager Craig for door hours, and Brick_haus demanding reimbursement for an Italian leather heel from a hostile hotel lobby.
